So calm on the outside doesn’t always feel calm to me lately, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or like you don’t have much energy lately, even when you haven’t been doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve clearly noticed that as its own thing, like, “Oh, I’m exhausted all the time even though I didn’t do anything.” I do have times where I feel kind of worn out, but I have a hard time separating whether that’s actual low energy or just feeling mentally drained from everything being on my mind.\n\nLike if I’ve been sitting around and I still don’t feel like getting up to do something, I don’t know if that’s because I’m physically tired or because I just don’t feel like dealing with it. I haven’t really been tracking it that closely. Some days I probably do feel more sluggish than I used to, but I can’t say for sure that I’m tired for no reason or anything like that. It’s more like… I feel worn down sometimes, I guess, but I don’t know if I’d call it no energy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's been harder to focus or concentrate on things lately, like following through on tasks or keeping your mind on what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. It’s not like I can’t understand what I’m doing or anything, but following through on things has definitely gotten harder. I’ll look at some paperwork or some job around the house that I normally would’ve just handled, and instead I kind of avoid it or put it off, and then it sits there in my head.\n\nAnd even when I’m doing something that’s supposed to clear my mind—like being out in the workshop or trying to fish—I’m still thinking about the situation at home. Since my father-in-law moved in, it’s like my attention keeps getting pulled back there. I’ll start one thing, then I’m thinking about some little house issue, or how tense things feel, or something I snapped at my wife about that I know wasn’t worth snapping over.\n\nSo, yeah… I guess my focus isn’t great lately. It feels less like I’m distracted by random things and more like my head just won’t quiet down enough for me to stay on task.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve been getting more easily frustrated or angry lately, even about small things that wouldn’t usually bother you?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, I would say that’s one of the main things that made me realize something wasn’t right. I’m here because ever since my father-in-law moved in, I’ve been snapping at my wife over these little house things that normally wouldn’t have gotten that kind of reaction from me. Like something is out of place, or something needs doing, or there’s some small thing around the house, and I hear myself getting sharp about it before I’ve even really thought it through.\n\nAnd then afterward I’m sitting there thinking, “Why did I make that into such a big deal?” It’s not really about the thing itself, I don’t think. It’s like I’m already wound up, so some little thing just becomes the thing I react to.\n\nI’ve also been putting off jobs and paperwork I used to be able to stay on top of, and that probably feeds into it too, because then I’m annoyed with myself, and everything feels backed up in my head. Even when I go out to the workshop or try to fish, which used to help me settle down, I can’t really get my head quiet. So by the time something small happens at home, I’m already right on the edge, and my wife ends up catching it, which I don’t feel good about.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your frustration or anger has sometimes felt out of proportion to the actual situation, like a small annoyance turning into a much bigger reaction than you intended?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that’s exactly it. It’s not that the thing itself is so terrible, you know? It’ll be something small around the house, something that normally I probably would’ve just dealt with or maybe barely noticed, and instead I hear myself snapping at my wife like it’s this huge issue. And then a little while later I’m thinking, “That really didn’t need to be that big.”\n\nI’m here because that pattern has gotten pretty obvious to me since my father-in-law moved in. I feel like I’m already wound up, so when some little house thing comes up, it turns into the thing I react to. And it’s not just the snapping, either—it’s like I’m not handling the normal stuff the way I used to. I’m putting off jobs and paperwork that I used to stay on top of, and then that hangs over me, and I think that probably makes me even more irritated.\n\nEven when I try to get away from it for a bit, like going out to the workshop or trying to fish, I can’t really get my head quiet. So by the time I’m back home, it doesn’t take much for me to overreact. I don’t like that, especially because my wife ends up getting the sharp side of it over things that really aren’t worth that kind of reaction.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your irritability, restlessness, and difficulty focusing only happen when you’re thinking about the situation at home, or do they sometimes come up even when you’re not focused on that stressor?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly tied to the situation at home, I think… but it doesn’t stay neatly contained there, if that makes sense. Like, I’m not just sitting there calmly and then only getting irritated the second I’m thinking, “Oh, my father-in-law moved in.” It’s more like that whole situation has me wound up in the background, and then it follows me around.\n\nI’m here because ever since he moved in, I’ve been snapping at my wife over little house things that I know I wouldn’t normally react to that way. And then that kind of spills over into other stuff too. I’ll put off jobs or paperwork I used to be able to stay on top of, and even if I’m not actively thinking about the house at that exact second, it’s like my mind isn’t settled enough to just do the thing in front of me.\n\nEven when I go out to the workshop or try to fish—which are usually the kinds of things that help me clear my head—I can’t really get my head quiet. I might be trying to focus on what I’m doing, but there’s still this tension running underneath it. So I’d say it starts with what’s going on at home, but it kind of comes with me even when I’m trying not to focus on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself getting more easily frustrated or restless in situations outside of the house, like at work or when you’re out running errands?\n\nPatient: Yeah… outside the house, I do notice it, but I’d say it’s still kind of connected to what’s going on at home. It’s not like I’m suddenly a different person everywhere for no reason. It’s more like I leave the house already carrying that tension with me.\n\nLike if I’m out in the workshop, that’s supposed to be where I can settle myself. Usually I can get focused on what I’m doing, use my hands, and it quiets things down. But lately I’ll be out there and my head just won’t stop. Same thing if I try to fish — I used to be able to just be there, you know, and now I’m still wound up. I can’t really get my head quiet.\n\nAnd I think that makes me more easily frustrated in general, even if I’m not actually at home in that moment. It’s been going on for about five months now, since shortly after my father-in-law moved into our house. It wasn’t like one bad day and then it cleared up. And then I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really about the thing I’m mad about. So… I wouldn’t say I don’t enjoy them at all. It’s not like I wake up and everything is fine and then I’m just low for no reason the whole day.\n\nSometimes it sort of follows me around more than I want it to, though. It’s more that since my father-in-law moved in, I feel like my head never really shuts off about the way things are at home.\n\nEven if nothing specific is happening right that second, I’ll be thinking about how cramped or tense things feel, or I’ll notice myself getting worked up over some little house thing that really shouldn’t matter that much. Then I snap at my wife about it, and afterward I’m thinking, “Why did I make that into such a big deal?” So the worry or agitation kind of spreads out, but it still feels like it comes from that situation.\n\nAnd I’m putting off things too—paperwork, jobs around the house, stuff I used to just handle—and that adds to it, because then it’s another thing sitting in the back of my mind. Even when I go out to the workshop or try to fish, which used to help me clear my head, I’m still thinking about everything. So… yeah, I guess I’m worried a lot, but it feels pretty connected to what’s been going on at home, not just for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling restless or on edge a lot of the time, like you can’t really relax even when things are calm?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably a fair way to put it. I don’t know that I’m pacing around all day or anything like that, but I do feel kind of wound up a lot of the time. Like I can sit down and the house might be quiet for a minute, but my head doesn’t really get quiet with it.\n\nEver since my father-in-law moved in, it’s like I’m always bracing for the next thing at home, or I’m noticing every little thing around the house. And then I’ll snap at my wife over something stupid, like some small house thing that normally I would’ve just dealt with or let go. Then afterward I feel bad because I know it wasn’t really about that one thing.\n\nEven when I try to get away from it a little—go out to the workshop or try to fish—it’s still in my head. I’ll be out there with the tools or sitting by the water, and instead of settling down, I’m thinking about what I’ve been putting off, paperwork or jobs around the house, or just how tense everything feels. So calm on the outside doesn’t always feel calm to me lately, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or like you don’t have much energy lately, even when you haven’t been doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve clearly noticed that as its own thing, like, “Oh, I’m exhausted all the time even though I didn’t do anything.” I do have times where I feel kind of worn out, but I have a hard time separating whether that’s actual low energy or just feeling mentally drained from everything being on my mind.\n\nLike if I’ve been sitting around and I still don’t feel like getting up to do something, I don’t know if that’s because I’m physically tired or because I just don’t feel like dealing with it. I haven’t really been tracking it that closely. Some days I probably do feel more sluggish than I used to, but I can’t say for sure that I’m tired for no reason or anything like that. It’s more like… I feel worn down sometimes, I guess, but I don’t know if I’d call it no energy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's been harder to focus or concentrate on things lately, like following through on tasks or keeping your mind on what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. It’s not like I can’t understand what I’m doing or anything, but following through on things has definitely gotten harder. I’ll look at some paperwork or some job around the house that I normally would’ve just handled, and instead I kind of avoid it or put it off, and then it sits there in my head.\n\nAnd even when I’m doing something that’s supposed to clear my mind—like being out in the workshop or trying to fish—I’m still thinking about the situation at home. Since my father-in-law moved in, it’s like my attention keeps getting pulled back there. I’ll start one thing, then I’m thinking about some little house issue, or how tense things feel, or something I snapped at my wife about that I know wasn’t worth snapping over.\n\nSo, yeah… I guess my focus isn’t great lately. It feels less like I’m distracted by random things and more like my head just won’t quiet down enough for me to stay on task.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve been getting more easily frustrated or angry lately, even about small things that wouldn’t usually bother you?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, I would say that’s one of the main things that made me realize something wasn’t right. I’m here because ever since my father-in-law moved in, I’ve been snapping at my wife over these little house things that normally wouldn’t have gotten that kind of reaction from me. Like something is out of place, or something needs doing, or there’s some small thing around the house, and I hear myself getting sharp about it before I’ve even really thought it through.\n\nAnd then afterward I’m sitting there thinking, “Why did I make that into such a big deal?” It’s not really about the thing itself, I don’t think. It’s like I’m already wound up, so some little thing just becomes the thing I react to.\n\nI’ve also been putting off jobs and paperwork I used to be able to stay on top of, and that probably feeds into it too, because then I’m annoyed with myself, and everything feels backed up in my head. Even when I go out to the workshop or try to fish, which used to help me settle down, I can’t really get my head quiet. So by the time something small happens at home, I’m already right on the edge, and my wife ends up catching it, which I don’t feel good about.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your frustration or anger has sometimes felt out of proportion to the actual situation, like a small annoyance turning into a much bigger reaction than you intended?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that’s exactly it. It’s not that the thing itself is so terrible, you know? It’ll be something small around the house, something that normally I probably would’ve just dealt with or maybe barely noticed, and instead I hear myself snapping at my wife like it’s this huge issue. And then a little while later I’m thinking, “That really didn’t need to be that big.”\n\nI’m here because that pattern has gotten pretty obvious to me since my father-in-law moved in. I feel like I’m already wound up, so when some little house thing comes up, it turns into the thing I react to. And it’s not just the snapping, either—it’s like I’m not handling the normal stuff the way I used to. I’m putting off jobs and paperwork that I used to stay on top of, and then that hangs over me, and I think that probably makes me even more irritated.\n\nEven when I try to get away from it for a bit, like going out to the workshop or trying to fish, I can’t really get my head quiet. So by the time I’m back home, it doesn’t take much for me to overreact. I don’t like that, especially because my wife ends up getting the sharp side of it over things that really aren’t worth that kind of reaction.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your irritability, restlessness, and difficulty focusing only happen when you’re thinking about the situation at home, or do they sometimes come up even when you’re not focused on that stressor?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly tied to the situation at home, I think… but it doesn’t stay neatly contained there, if that makes sense. Like, I’m not just sitting there calmly and then only getting irritated the second I’m thinking, “Oh, my father-in-law moved in.” It’s more like that whole situation has me wound up in the background, and then it follows me around.\n\nI’m here because ever since he moved in, I’ve been snapping at my wife over little house things that I know I wouldn’t normally react to that way. And then that kind of spills over into other stuff too. I’ll put off jobs or paperwork I used to be able to stay on top of, and even if I’m not actively thinking about the house at that exact second, it’s like my mind isn’t settled enough to just do the thing in front of me.\n\nEven when I go out to the workshop or try to fish—which are usually the kinds of things that help me clear my head—I can’t really get my head quiet. I might be trying to focus on what I’m doing, but there’s still this tension running underneath it. So I’d say it starts with what’s going on at home, but it kind of comes with me even when I’m trying not to focus on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself getting more easily frustrated or restless in situations outside of the house, like at work or when you’re out running errands?\n\nPatient: Yeah… outside the house, I do notice it, but I’d say it’s still kind of connected to what’s going on at home. It’s not like I’m suddenly a different person everywhere for no reason. It’s more like I leave the house already carrying that tension with me.\n\nLike if I’m out in the workshop, that’s supposed to be where I can settle myself. Usually I can get focused on what I’m doing, use my hands, and it quiets things down. But lately I’ll be out there and my head just won’t stop. Same thing if I try to fish — I used to be able to just be there, you know, and now I’m still wound up. I can’t really get my head quiet.\n\nAnd I think that makes me more easily frustrated in general, even if I’m not actually at home in that moment. I’m here because since my father-in-law moved in, I’ve been snapping at my wife over little house things, and I’ve been putting off jobs and paperwork I used to keep up with. So even when I’m away from the house, it feels like I’m still behind or still bracing for something, and that restlessness comes with me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you sometimes feel irritable or restless even when you’re not thinking about the situation at home or any other specific stressor?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, but it’s hard for me to separate it out cleanly. It’s not always like I’m actively thinking, “This is because of the situation at home,” or specifically thinking about my father-in-law being there. Sometimes I’m just… already on edge. Like my body is still carrying it even if my mind isn’t directly on it.\n\nI’m here because ever since my father-in-law moved in, I’ve been snapping at my wife over these little house things that honestly shouldn’t set me off the way they do. And then I’m putting off jobs and paperwork I used to just handle, and that makes me feel more unsettled too. Even when I go out to the workshop or try to fish, which used to be my way of clearing my head, I can’t seem to get quiet inside. My thoughts keep circling, or I’m restless, or I just can’t settle into what I’m doing.\n\nSo, yeah, sometimes I’m irritable or restless even when I’m not consciously thinking about any one specific stressor. But it still feels like it all started with what changed at home, and now it’s just kind of following me around.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you sometimes feel irritable or restless for no clear reason, even when nothing specific is bothering you or stressing you out?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes it feels like there isn’t one clear thing in that exact moment, but I don’t know that I’d say it’s for no reason. It’s more like I’m carrying this tension around with me, even when I’m not actively thinking about it.\n\nI mean, I’m here because ever since my father-in-law moved in, I’ve been snapping at my wife over little house things — stuff I know shouldn’t bother me that much. And I’ve been putting off jobs and paperwork I used to stay on top of without making such a big deal out of it. Even when I go out to the workshop or try to fish, which usually helps me settle down, I just can’t seem to get my head quiet.\n\nSo if you asked me in the moment, “What’s bothering you right now?” I might not always have a neat answer. But it still feels connected to everything at home, like that stress is sitting in the background and making me restless or irritable even when nothing specific is happening right then.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your irritability and restlessness have been present almost every day since this situation started with your father-in-law moving in, or do they come and go depending on what’s happening?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on for about five months now, since shortly after my father-in-law moved into our house. It wasn’t like one bad day and then it cleared up. It’s more like… once that change happened, the irritability and restlessness started showing up and they’ve been around pretty regularly since then.\n\nI don’t know if I’d say every single minute of every day, because there are moments where I’m distracted or things are calmer, but overall it’s been a pretty steady thing across these months. It comes and goes in intensity depending on what’s happening, but it hasn’t really gone away since it started.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
